\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{aligned}\,

it holds that
(\nu\circ\tau)(\mu\circ\sigma)=(\nu\mu)\circ(\tau\sigma)
.

Proof.

For a class
[s^{\prime},t^{\prime}]\in(T^{\prime}\circ S^{\prime})_{c,a}
we define

\begin{split}\langle s^{\prime},t^{\prime}\rangle_{b}&=\left\{\left(\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.29861pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}\hskip 1.00006pt,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{7.22223pt}{6.15079pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}\hskip 1.00006pt\right)\in S^{\prime}_{b,a}\times T^{\prime}_{c,b}~|~\left(\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.29861pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}\hskip 1.00006pt,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{7.22223pt}{6.15079pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}\hskip 1.00006pt\right)\sim(s^{\prime},t^{\prime})\right\}\\
&=\left\{\left(\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.29861pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}\hskip 1.00006pt,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{7.22223pt}{6.15079pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}\hskip 1.00006pt\right)\,|\,\exists b^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}b\,:\,\left(\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.29861pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}\hskip 1.00006pt.f,f^{-1}.\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{7.22223pt}{6.15079pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}\hskip 1.00006pt\right)=(s^{\prime},t^{\prime})\right\}\\
&=[s^{\prime},t^{\prime}]\cap(S^{\prime}_{b,a}\times T^{\prime}_{c,b})\end{split}

(29)

By the orbit-stabilizer theorem and the fact that our profunctors act freely we can express horizontal composition (
22
) as follows:

\tau\circ\sigma\left([s,t],[s^{\prime},t^{\prime}]\right):=\hskip-5.0pt\sum_{(\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{6.79517pt}{3.01389pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}\hskip 0.81949pt,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{6.04169pt}{4.30556pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}\hskip 0.81949pt)\in\langle s^{\prime},t^{\prime}\rangle_{b}}\hskip-5.0pt\sigma\left(s,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.29861pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}\hskip 1.00006pt\right)\tau\left(t,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{7.22223pt}{6.15079pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}\hskip 1.00006pt\right).

(30)

Then, for
s\in S_{ba,},t\in T_{c,b},s^{\prime\prime}\in S^{\prime\prime}_{b^{\prime},a},t^{\prime\prime}\in T^{\prime\prime}_{c,b^{\prime}}
, the following holds:

\begin{split}(\nu\circ\tau)&(\mu\circ\sigma)([s,t],[s^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime}])\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:vertcomp}}}}{{=}}\hskip-10.00002pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{classes}\\[2.84526pt]
[s^{\prime},t^{\prime}]\in(T^{\prime}\circ S^{\prime})_{c,a}\end{subarray}}\hskip-15.00002pt(\mu\circ\sigma)([s,t],[s^{\prime},t^{\prime}])(\nu\circ\tau)([s^{\prime},t^{\prime}],[s^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime}])\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:horcomp2}}}}{{=}}\hskip-10.00002pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{classes}\\[2.84526pt]
[s^{\prime},t^{\prime}]\in(T^{\prime}\circ S^{\prime})_{c,a}\\
(\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{6.79517pt}{3.01389pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}\hskip 0.81949pt,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{6.04169pt}{4.30556pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}\hskip 0.81949pt)\in\langle s^{\prime},t^{\prime}\rangle_{b}\end{subarray}}\hskip-15.00002pt\sigma(s,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.29861pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}\hskip 1.00006pt)\mu(t,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{7.22223pt}{6.15079pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}\hskip 1.00006pt)(\nu\circ\tau)([s^{\prime},t^{\prime}],[s^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime}])\\
&=\hskip-8.00003pt\sum_{\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{6.79517pt}{3.01389pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}\hskip 0.81949pt\in S_{b,a},\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{6.04169pt}{4.30556pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}\hskip 0.81949pt\in T_{c,b}}\hskip-8.99994pt\sigma(s,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.29861pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}\hskip 1.00006pt)\mu(t,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{7.22223pt}{6.15079pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}\hskip 1.00006pt)(\nu\circ\tau)([\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.29861pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}\hskip 1.00006pt,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{7.22223pt}{6.15079pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}\hskip 1.00006pt],[s^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime}])\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:horcomp2}}}}{{=}}\hskip-8.00003pt\sum_{\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{6.79517pt}{3.01389pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}\hskip 0.81949pt\in S_{b,a},\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{6.04169pt}{4.30556pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}\hskip 0.81949pt\in T_{c,b}}\hskip-8.99994pt\sigma(s,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.29861pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}\hskip 1.00006pt)\mu(t,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{7.22223pt}{6.15079pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}\hskip 1.00006pt)\hskip-10.00002pt\sum_{(\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{5.55557pt}{7.22223pt}}}\smash{\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{6.79517pt}{3.01389pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}}}\hskip 0.81949pt,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{5.55557pt}{7.22223pt}}}\smash{\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{6.04169pt}{4.30556pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}}}\hskip 0.81949pt)\in\langle s^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\rangle_{b}}\hskip-10.00002pt\tau(\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.29861pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}\hskip 1.00006pt,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{5.55557pt}{7.22223pt}}}\smash{\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.29861pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt)\nu(\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{7.22223pt}{6.15079pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}\hskip 1.00006pt,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{5.55557pt}{7.22223pt}}}\smash{\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{7.22223pt}{6.15079pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:vertcomp}}}}{{=}}\hskip-8.00003pt\sum_{(\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{5.55557pt}{7.22223pt}}}\smash{\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{6.79517pt}{3.01389pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}}}\hskip 0.81949pt,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{5.55557pt}{7.22223pt}}}\smash{\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{6.04169pt}{4.30556pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}}}\hskip 0.81949pt)\in\langle s^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\rangle_{b}}\hskip-8.99994pt\tau\sigma(s,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{5.55557pt}{7.22223pt}}}\smash{\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.29861pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}s}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt)\nu\mu(t,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{5.55557pt}{7.22223pt}}}\smash{\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{7.22223pt}{6.15079pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{t}t}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:horcomp2}}}}{{=}}\left((\nu\mu)\circ(\tau\sigma)\right)([s,t],[s^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime}])\end{split}

Associator.

Let
P:\mathbf{A}{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}\mathbf{B},~Q:\mathbf{B}{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}\mathbf{C},~R:\mathbf{C}{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}\mathbf{D}
be profunctors. It is well known that there are natural isomorphisms (and hence proisomorphisms)

\alpha_{P,Q,R}:R\circ(Q\circ P)\xRightarrow{}(R\circ Q)\circ P

fulfilling the pentagon equation. In our notation,

\alpha_{P,Q,R}([[p,q],r])=[p,[q,r]]

(31)

for
p\in P_{b,a},q\in Q_{c,b}
and
r\in R_{d,c}
. It remains to show that
\alpha
remains natural in
P,Q,R
after extending to
\mathbf{GpdAct_{f}}.

Claim.

\alpha_{P,Q,R}
is natural in
P,Q,R
.

Proof.

Let
\sigma:P{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}P^{\prime},\tau:Q{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}Q^{\prime}
and
\mu:R{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}R^{\prime}
be protransformations.
Then, the following holds for
p\in P_{b,a},q\in Q_{c,b},r\in R_{d,c},p^{\prime}\in P_{b^{\prime},a},q^{\prime}\in Q_{c^{\prime},b^{\prime}},r^{\prime}\in R_{d,c^{\prime}}
:

\begin{split}&\left((\mu\circ\tau)\circ\sigma\right)\alpha_{P,Q,R}([[p,q],r],[p^{\prime},[q^{\prime},r^{\prime}]])\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:defassociator}}}}{{=}}((\mu\circ\tau)\circ\sigma)([p,[q,r]],[p^{\prime},[q^{\prime},r^{\prime}]])\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:horcomp1}}}}{{=}}\textstyle\sum_{b^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}b}\sigma(p.f,p^{\prime})(\mu\circ\tau)([q,r],f.[q^{\prime},r^{\prime}])\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:horcomp1}}}}{{=}}\textstyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\xrightarrow{}}}b\\
c^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g}c\end{subarray}}\hskip-1.99997pt\sigma(p.f,p^{\prime})\tau(q.g,f.q^{\prime})\mu(r,g.r^{\prime})\end{split}

while

\begin{split}&\alpha_{P^{\prime},Q^{\prime},R^{\prime}}\left(\mu\circ(\tau\circ\sigma)\right)([[p,q],r],[p^{\prime},[q^{\prime},r^{\prime}]])\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:defassociator}}}}{{=}}(\mu\circ(\tau\circ\sigma))([[p,q],r],[[p^{\prime},q^{\prime}],r^{\prime}])\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:horcomp1}}}}{{=}}\textstyle\sum_{c^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g}c}(\tau\circ\sigma)([p,q].g,[p^{\prime},q^{\prime}])\mu(r,g.r^{\prime})\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:horcomp1}}}}{{=}}\textstyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}b\\
c^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g}c\end{subarray}}\sigma(p.f,p^{\prime})\tau(q.g,f.q^{\prime})\mu(r,g.r^{\prime})\end{split}

Unitors.

For every groupoid
\mathbf{A}
, we define the identity profunctor

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbf{A}}:\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathbf{A}\xrightarrow{}\mathbf{Set}\hskip 14.22636pta,a^{\prime}\mapsto\mathrm{Hom}(a,a^{\prime}).

Let
P:\mathbf{A}{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}\mathbf{B}
be a profunctor. It is well known that there are natural isomorphisms (and thus proisomorphisms)
\lambda_{P}:P\circ\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbf{A}}{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}P
and
\rho_{P}:\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbf{B}}\circ P{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}P
fulfilling the triangle equations. In our notation

\lambda_{P}([f,p]):=f.p\hskip 21.33955pt\rho_{P}([p,g])=p.g

(32)

for
f\in\mathrm{Hom}(a,a^{\prime}),~p\in P_{b,a},~g\in\mathrm{Hom}(b^{\prime},b)
.

Claim.

\rho_{P}
and
\lambda_{P}
are natural in
P
.

Proof.
Let
\sigma:P{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}Q
be a protransformation. For
f\in\mathrm{Hom}(a,a^{\prime}),p\in P_{b,a}
and
q\in Q_{b,a^{\prime}}
we have

\sigma\lambda_{P}([f,p],q)=\sigma(f.p,q)

and

\begin{split}&\lambda_{Q}(\sigma\circ 1_{\mathbbm{1}_{A}})([f,p],q)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:vertcomp}}}}{{=}}\hskip-18.00005pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{classes}\\
[g,q^{\prime}]\in(Q\circ\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbf{A}})_{b,a^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\hskip-15.00002pt(\sigma\circ 1_{\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbf{A}}})([f,p],[g,q^{\prime}])\,\lambda_{Q}([g,q^{\prime}],q)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:horcomp2}}}}{{=}}\hskip-18.00005pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{classes}\\
[g,q^{\prime}]\in(Q\circ\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbf{A}})_{b,a^{\prime}}\\
(\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.3049pt}{3.01389pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{g}g}}\hskip 0.81949pt,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.06879pt}{3.01389pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{g}q}}\hskip 0.81949pt)\in\langle g,q^{\prime}\rangle_{a}\end{subarray}}\hskip-15.00002pt1_{\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbf{A}}}(f,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{10.25693pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{g}g}}\hskip 1.00006pt)\,\sigma(p,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{9.95137pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{g}q}}\hskip 1.00006pt)\,\lambda_{Q}([\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{10.25693pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{g}g}}\hskip 1.00006pt,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{9.95137pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{g}q}}\hskip 1.00006pt],q)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:defunitor}}}}{{=}}\hskip-8.99994pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.3049pt}{3.01389pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{g}g}}\hskip 0.81949pt\in\mathrm{Hom}(a,a^{\prime})\\
\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.06879pt}{3.01389pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{g}q}}\hskip 0.81949pt\in Q_{b,a}\end{subarray}}\delta_{f,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.3049pt}{3.01389pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{g}g}}\hskip 0.81949pt}\,\sigma(p,\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{10.25693pt}{4.30554pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{g}g}}\hskip 1.00006pt)\,\delta_{\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.3049pt}{3.01389pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{g}g}}\hskip 0.81949pt.\widetilde{\mathrlap{\phantom{\rule{8.06879pt}{3.01389pt}}}\smash{\vphantom{g}q}}\hskip 0.81949pt,q}\\
&=\sigma(p,f^{-1}.q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\tiny\eqref{eq:propprotrafo}}}}{{=}}\sigma(f.p,q)=\sigma\lambda_{P}([f,p],q)\end{split}

A similar proof shows that
\rho_{P}
is natural in
P
.
∎

\theoremdaggerpivotal

*

Proof.

We first consider the dagger structure, then the pivotal structure.

Dagger.

From the symmetry of the expressions (
23
) and (
22
) we conclude that
\left(\sigma\tau\right)^{\dagger}=\tau^{\dagger}\sigma^{\dagger}
and
\left(\sigma\circ\tau\right)^{\dagger}=\sigma^{\dagger}\circ\tau^{\dagger}
. All invertible natural transformations and in particular all coherence isomorphisms are unitary.

Pivotality.

We show that for every profunctor
P:\mathbf{A}{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}\mathbf{B}
, the cup (
26
) and cap (
25
) are well defined. We first show that
\epsilon
descends to a map on the quotient. Let
p\in P_{b,a},p^{\prime}\in P_{b,a^{\prime}}
,
q\in P_{b^{\prime},a},q^{\prime}\in P_{b^{\prime},a^{\prime}}
and
f\in\mathrm{Hom}(a^{\prime},a)
and suppose that
(p,p^{\prime})\sim(q,q^{\prime})
. Then, there is a
b\xrightarrow{r}b^{\prime}
s.t.
(p.r,r^{-1}\star p^{\prime})=(q,q^{\prime})
, where
r^{-1}\star p^{\prime}=p^{\prime}.r
is the dual action. Therefore

\epsilon([q,q^{\prime}],f)=\delta_{f^{-1}.q,q^{\prime}}=\delta_{f^{-1}.p.r,p^{\prime}.r}=\delta_{f^{-1}.p,p^{\prime}}.

To show that
\epsilon
is an intertwiner we let
p,p^{\prime}
and
f
be as above and let
a\xrightarrow{g}\widetilde{a}
and
\widetilde{a}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{h}a^{\prime}
. Therefore,

\epsilon(g.[p,p^{\prime}].h,gfh)=\delta_{h^{-1}f^{-1}g^{-1}g.p,h^{-1}.p^{\prime}}=\delta_{f^{-1}.p,p^{\prime}}.

Using unitors and associators, we can show that the left transpose of any morphism
\mu:P{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}S
, given by the composite

\left(\epsilon\circ 1_{P*}\right)\left(1_{S^{*}}\circ\mu\circ 1_{P*}\right)\left(1_{S*}\circ\eta\right),

is equal to the protransformation
\mu^{*}:S^{*}{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}P^{*}
defined by the functions

S_{a,b}\times P_{a,b}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{swap}}P_{a,b}\times S_{a,b}\xrightarrow{\mu}\mathbb{N}.

For
\mu=1_{P}:P{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}P
this implies the snake equation. A similar argument shows that the right tranpose is given by the same equation. This proves that our 2-category is a dagger pivotal 2-category.
∎

\theorempseudofunctor

*

Proof (sketch).

Here, we use the description of
\mathbf{C^{*}Alg}
in terms of symmetric separable dagger Frobenius algebras, Frobenius bimodules and intertwiners
[
38
,
21
,
17
]
.
We first show that the data given in
4.7
is well defined:
Given a profunctor
P:\mathbf{A}{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}\mathbf{B}
, the vector space
\bigoplus_{b,a}\mathbb{C}P_{b,a}
is a
\mathbb{C}\mathbf{A}{-}\mathbb{C}\mathbf{B}
bimodule with action

\mathbb{C}\mathbf{A}\otimes\bigoplus_{b,a}\mathbb{C}P_{b,a}\otimes\mathbb{C}\mathbf{B}\xrightarrow{}\bigoplus_{b,a}\mathbb{C}P_{b,a}

induced from the action (
19
) of the morphisms on
P_{b,a}
(again defined to be
0
on non-compatible components).

Given a protransformation
\sigma:P{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}T
, the linear map
Q(\sigma)
is indeed an intertwiner: For
p\in P_{b,a},a\xrightarrow{f}a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g}b
:

\begin{split}Q(\sigma)(f.p.g)&=\sum_{t\in T_{b,a}}\sigma(f.p.g,t)t=\sum_{t\in T_{b,a}}\sigma(p,f^{-1}.t.g^{-1})t\\
&=\sum_{\widetilde{t}\in T_{b,a}}\sigma(p,\widetilde{t})f.\widetilde{t}.g=f.Q(\sigma).g\end{split}

By definition,
Q
preserves the dagger structure.

The critical step in the proof of functoriality is showing that the horizontal composition of 2-morphisms is preserved.
Formally, we defined horizontal composition of profunctors
P:\mathbf{A}{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}\mathbf{B}
and
S:\mathbf{B}{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{}}}\mathbf{C}
and protransformations via the coequalizer of the maps

\displaystyle\textstyle\coprod_{b,b^{\prime}}P_{b^{\prime},a}\times\mathrm{Hom}(b,b^{\prime})\times S_{c,b}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{}\textstyle\coprod_{b}P_{b,a}\times S_{c,b}

\displaystyle(p,f,s)

\displaystyle\mapsto(p.f,s)

\displaystyle(p,f,s)

\displaystyle\mapsto(p,f.s)

leading to the equivalence relation
(p,s)\sim(p.f,f^{-1}.s)
.
We denoted this coequalizer as follows:

\epsilon:\coprod_{b\in\mathrm{ob}\mathbf{B}}P_{b,a}\times S_{c,b}\xrightarrow{}\left(S\circ P\right)_{c,a}\hskip 28.45274pt(p,s)\mapsto[p,s]

In
\mathbf{C^{*}Alg}
, the horizontal composition of
Q(P)
and
Q(S)
are defined via the splitting of the dagger idempotent
[
21
]

X\in\mathrm{End}\left(\bigoplus_{b,a}\mathbb{C}P_{b,a}\otimes\bigoplus_{c,\widetilde{b}}\mathbb{C}S_{c,\widetilde{b}}\right)
given at stage
((b,a,c,\widetilde{b}),(b^{\prime},a^{\prime},c^{\prime},\widetilde{b}^{\prime}))
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}P_{b,a}\otimes\mathbb{C}S_{c,\widetilde{b}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{}\mathbb{C}P_{b^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbb{C}S_{c^{\prime},\widetilde{b}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle(p,s)

\displaystyle\mapsto\frac{\delta_{b,\widetilde{b}}\delta_{b^{\prime},\widetilde{b}^{\prime}}\delta_{a,a^{\prime}}\delta_{c,c^{\prime}}}{|\mathrm{Hom}(b^{\prime},b)|}\sum_{b^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}b}(p.f,f^{-1}.s)

To show that our functor preserves horizontal composition we thus have to show that the quantization of the coequalizer
Q(\epsilon):(p,s)\mapsto[p,s]
splits
X
(up to a constant). And indeed,

Q(\epsilon)^{\dagger}Q(\epsilon)\in\mathrm{End}\left(\bigoplus_{a,b,c}\mathbb{C}P_{b,a}\otimes\mathbb{C}S_{c,b}\right)

is given at stage
((a,b,c),(a^{\prime},b^{\prime},c^{\prime})
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}P_{b,a}\otimes\mathbb{C}S_{c,b}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{}\mathbb{C}P_{b^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbb{C}S_{c^{\prime},b^{\prime}}

\displaystyle(p,s)

\displaystyle\mapsto\delta_{a,a^{\prime}}\delta_{c,c^{\prime}}\sum_{(\widetilde{p},\widetilde{s})\in\langle p,s\rangle_{b^{\prime}}}(\widetilde{p},\widetilde{s})

\displaystyle=\delta_{a,a^{\prime}}\delta_{c,c^{\prime}}\frac{1}{|\mathrm{St}(p,s)|}\sum_{b^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f}b}(p.f,f^{-1}.s)

where
\mathrm{St}(p,s)=\left\{b\xrightarrow{g}b~|~(p.g,g^{-1}.s)=(p,s)\right\}
is the stabilizer of the joint action of
\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathbf{B}}(b)
on
P_{b,a}\times S_{c,b}
and in
\langle p,s\rangle_{b^{\prime}}
is defined in (
29
).
Since all groupoids in
\mathbf{GpdAct_{f}}
act freely, this stabilizer vanishes and
Q(\epsilon)^{\dagger}Q(\epsilon)
is indeed proportional to
X
. Similarly
Q(\epsilon)Q(\epsilon)^{\dagger}
is proportional to
\mathbbm{1}_{Q(S\circ P)}
. ∎
